Another thought: The objective can be expressed in terms of a polynomial P(z) with nonnegative coefficients. We want coefficients of P(z)^2 ≤ T. This is a polynomial inequality. There are known "flat-top" finite filter designs: Chebyshev polynomials can make first n coefficients equal and tail minimal; the q recurrence is one such. Implement active set descent:
- Given p, compute c via FFT, max M.
- Choose weights w: top L lags with c close to max: w_k ∝ (c_k/M)^β, or uniform on top. β anneal.
- Compute h=H(w)p.
- Candidate p_new = p * exp(-eta * h / scale) renormalized. Need choose eta. Scale h to avoid large changes: scale = max(h) or std. Use eta=0.1 etc. Accept if objective lower; else reduce/try random.
- We can do many iterations. This is essentially fractional programming and may plateau.

Can also use uphill SA: accept worse with probability exp(-Δ/T). Time budget. But objective may be landscape with discrete valley.

Other moves: coordinate/position moves: swap weights between positions (preserving sum) to rearrange mass. For nonconvex, rearrangement important. A move: choose i,j, transfer ε mass: p_i' =p_i+δ, p_j'=p_j-δ. Evaluate objective. This can shift mass along sequence. We can do random pair optimization: for a pair, choose transfer that roughly minimizes objective along interval (parabolic in δ for active lag?). Could be effective to move mass to better positions.

We can implement a local search with:
- Block scaling multiplicative as previous but more active-weighted.
- Pair transfers: pick two indices, evaluate few δ values via exact objective (or approximate). Since n maybe <=512, objective FFT cheap. Many moves per second.
- Random restarts param and lengths.

Coordinate update with sum fixed can move mass from one lag to another, potentially important. We can implement a stochastic coordinate search:
- Choose two indices i,j.
- Let E=p_i+p_j.
- Candidate x values: E * ratios [0.0,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9,0.95,0.98,1.0] and around current p_i maybe.
- For each candidate, set p_i=x, p_j=E-x, evaluate objective (FFT). Keep best.
This is O(num_candidates * FFT) per move. For n=512, 10 candidates FFT 0.002s =>0.02s per move, too many (1000 moves 20s ok). Could use exact convolve? FFT. Many moves possible.

This pair redistribution can shape sequence: moves heavy mass to positions where it doesn't create high sums. Starting from recurrence or random. Also random block permutation? But nonconvex landscape. Let's design a search engine:
- Maintain best sequences for several lengths n.
- For each active sequence, perform iterations:
   * Multiplicative active descent (can make smooth improvements).
   * Pair transfer: choose pair (i,j), E; choose a random λ with distribution biased to create new patterns: λ = rng.beta(a,b) maybe. Set p_i=λE,p_j=(1-λ)E. Evaluate; if improve or accept SA, update.
   * Occasionally block swap: choose two blocks and swap (preserving multiset) or permute a set of random indices.
- Keep global best.
- Time allocate to most promising lengths/scores.
- At end, evaluate best with exact evaluator (provided) to ensure valid.

Let's define functions:
- `fft_conv_sq(a)`: returns full convolution. Use size = 1; while size < 2n-1: size <<=1? Better use `scipy.fft.next_fast_len` if available; else power of two. To avoid scipy import maybe numpy FFT power-of-two enough. n up to 2048, size 4096. Use `numpy.fft.rfft`.
```
def conv_sq(a):
    n=a.size
    if n==0 return np.array([0.0])
    m=2*n-1
    size=1
    while size<m: size*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, size)
    c=np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, size)[:m]
    # clip negative tiny
    c[c<0]=0.0
    return c
```
For n small maybe direct? Not necessary.
- `score_from_conv(c, s=1.0)`: if s is sum? We'll maintain p sum1. score=2*n*maxc/s^2. But length n known. For unnormalized, pass sum.
```
def objective(p):
   p=np.asarray(p); n=p.size; S=p.sum(); if S<=0 return inf; c=conv_sq(p); maxc=c.max(); return 2*n*maxc/(S*S)
```
Exact oracle `evaluate_sequence` can be called at final. Use own objective for speed; should match.

```
   size=next pow2 >= 2n + m? p len n, w_rev len m, convolution len n+m-1=3n-2? But we only need indices L=2n-2 - j for j=0..n-1. The full conv of p (len n) and w_rev (len m) length n+m-1=3n-2. Need size >=3n-2. However w indices j+l up to 2n-2, and p len n; full conv needed. Use size>=3n-2.
```
Let's derive: h_j=∑_{l=0}^{n-1} w_{j+l} p_l, j=0..n-1. w length 2n-1. This is cross-correlation. Using convolution with w_rev length 2n-1, result index L-j where L=2n-2. Valid conv index range 0..n+(2n-1)-1=3n-2; L-j from L-(n-1)=n-1 to L=2n-2, within. Need size >=3n-2. Use power-of-two.
```
def hankel_mult(p,w):
   n=p.size; L=2*n-2
   size=1; while size < 3*n-2: size*=2
   fr=np.fft.rfft(p,size); fw=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],size)
   cr=np.fft.irfft(fr*fw,size)
   return cr[L - np.arange(n)]  # cr[n-1 .. 2n-2]?
```
Check j=0 -> index L =2n-2; j=n-1 -> L-(n-1)=n-1. Good.

Local search: We can implement a deterministic improvement using pair moves. It should not be too slow.
```
def local_search(p, budget_s, seed):
   rng=np.random.default_rng(seed)
   p=np.asarray(p,dtype=float); p=np.clip(p,0,1000); p/=p.sum()
   n=p.size
   current_score=objective_arr(p); best=p.copy(); best_score=current_score
   deadline=time.time()+budget_s
   temperature=0.02*best_score
   stale=0
   while time.time()<deadline:
      move_type=rng.random()
      if move_type<0.7: pair transfer
      elif <0.85: block scaling
      elif <0.95: multiplicative gradient
      else: random permutation/swap
      candidate=...
      sc=objective_arr(candidate)
      if finite and (sc<current_score or (temperature>0 and rng.random()<np.exp(-(sc-current_score)/temperature))):
           current_score=sc; p=candidate
           if sc<best_score: best...; stale=0; temperature*=0.999
      else: stale+=1
      temperature=max(1e-4*best_score, temperature*0.9995)
      if stale>10000: break/restart? 
   return best
```
For pair transfer, one evaluation per loop. For n=1024, one loop ~0.004s -> 600s=150k moves. Good. For n=256, more.
Block scaling: choose width max(2, n//32), start, factor exp(normal(0,0.3)), renorm.
Multiplicative gradient: like stage but on arbitrary p:
   c=conv_sq(p); M; weights; h; 
   p_candidate = p * np.exp(-eta * h / max(h)) normalized. Need eta. p may have zeros; h maybe zeros. Use eta=0.1, also try 0.3? One eval.
Random moves: swap two values or random p from Dirichlet? Swapping preserves multiset. Could rerandomize occasionally? Maybe not.

Be careful if p has zeros; pair transfer can create zeros. Multiplicative update with zeros cannot increase zeros (p_i * factor stays zero). To allow zeros to become positive, use a small floor: p = (p + floor)/(1+n*floor) before smoothing? But zeros are allowed; optimum may have zeros? Probably not, but to escape, we can occasionally add tiny noise: p_candidate = p + 1e-6*rng.random(n), renorm. Add with prob 0.01.

One issue: In harmonic optimizer, current score is based on softmax p (sum1). The objective is scale invariant. But our gradient with weights w and `mg=p*gp` for parameters a where v=B@a. If p=softmax(v), ∂p_i/∂a = p_i(B_i a coefficient) - p_i p (mean term). Since objective scale invariant, the p_i*p term contribution to grad is constant? F scalar homogeneous degree0: ∂F/∂p · (p p)? = p_j∂F/∂p_j * p_i? The correction vector is p*p (rank1 constant with components p_i p_j). Dot with constant direction? The correction from softmax Jacobian is - p_i * (B_j a_j) p_i? Actually ∂p_i/∂a_l = p_i (b_{il} - ∑ p_j b_{jl}). grad_a_l =∑ ∂F/∂p_i p_i (b_{il} - mean_l). The second term = - mean_l ∑ ∂F/∂p_i p_i. Since F homogeneous degree0, ∑ ∂F/∂p_i p_i =0 (Euler). Thus correction vanishes. Good. So grad_a=B.T@(p*grad_p) is exact for smooth F. Nice.

For nonsmooth exact, semi-gradient. Good.

We should maybe center basis columns? If basis columns have nonzero mean, softmax correction still vanishes due homogeneity, and constant direction not represented? If basis has nonzero mean, effectively includes constant shift; But adding a constant to v doesn't change p? Wait p=exp(v)/sum is invariant under adding any constant to all components, not just basis. If a basis vector has nonzero mean, changing it effectively adds nonconstant plus constant; fine. `make_fourier_basis`:
```
def make_fourier_basis(n, max_freq):
    i=np.arange(n)
    cols=[]
    for m in range(1,max_freq+1):
       angle=2*np.pi*m*i/n
       cols.append(np.cos(angle))
       cols.append(np.sin(angle))
    if not cols: return np.zeros((n,0))
    return np.column_stack(cols)
```
D=2*max_freq.

Objective function with invalid:
```
def objective_arr(p):
   p=np.asarray(p,dtype=float)
   if p.size==0: return np.inf
   p=np.clip(p,0.0,1000.0)
   S=p.sum()
   if S<=1e-12: return np.inf
   c=conv_sq(p)
   M=c.max()
   if not np.isfinite(M): return np.inf
   return float(2.0*p.size*M/(S*S))
```

```
   e=p[i]+p[j]

   elif rng.random()<0.5: lam=p[i]/e
   else: lam=rng.choice(np.array([0.,0.01,0.05,0.1,0.25,0.5,0.75,0.9,0.95,0.99,1.0])) # rng.choice with array each loop costly; precompute? but okay?

   start = int(rng.integers(0,max(1,n-width+1)))

      i,j=rng.integers(n,2); cand=p.copy(); cand[i],cand[j]=p[j],p[i]
   else:
      cand=p+p*1e-6*rng.random(n); cand/cand.sum()
```

```
   idx=np.arange(n)

   # vectorize: return (np.arange(n)[:,None] + np.arange(n)[None,:])? Need w index matrix n x n

   return w[indices].dot(p) # w[indices] n x n times p
```
Memory for n=128 16k okay. But for many calls in optimization, direct O(n^2) maybe okay for n<=128. We'll implement direct for n<=128 to avoid FFT overhead and index. For n=128, 16k operations per call, okay. For n=256, FFT.
```
def hankel_mult(p,w):
  n=p.size
  if n <= 128:
     idx = np.add.outer(np.arange(n), np.arange(n))
     return w[idx].dot(p)
  else: FFT
```
But creating idx each call expensive. Could precompute? no closure. For n small and many calls, maybe use loops? But local search small n maybe. Better use FFT for all n to keep code simple. FFT overhead for n=64 small? okay. Use FFT all.

But in DT local search for n=64, FFT maybe okay. We'll just use FFT.

`conv_sq` direct for n<=128 to reduce rounding? Use np.convolve.

Potential memory in FFT: size power of two for n=1024 ->4096; fine.

Time printing: print with flush? Not necessary.

```
def fourier_optimize(n, max_freq, budget_s, seed):
    start=time.time(); deadline=start+budget_s
    B=make_fourier_basis(n,max_freq)
    D=B.shape[1]
    if D==0: return None, inf

    max_norm=float(4.0+0.2*max_freq)
    for restart in range(30):
       if time.time()>deadline: break
       a=rng.normal(0.0, 0.25, D)
       m_state=np.zeros(D); v_state=np.zeros(D); step=0.15
       stale=0

       while True:

          v=B.dot(a); p=...; 
          c=conv_sq(p); M=c.max(); score=2*n*M
          if score<best_score: best...

          # compute weights
          ...

          if np.max(np.abs(grad)) < 1e-12: stale+=100
          else: ...
          # update
          denom = np.sqrt(v_state)+1e-8
          for mult in [1.0,0.5,0.25,0.1]:
              a_try = a - step*mult*m_state/denom
              norm = np.linalg.norm(a_try)
              if norm>max_norm: a_try *= max_norm/norm

       if best_score <= 1.505: break? Maybe return.
```

Scoring current p after update not recomputed? We update score. Good.
But line search uses `score` exact current before update; if no accepted, we don't change p. Next iteration recompute (same) may waste. Could after no accepted, break restart after stale.

```python
"""
Search for nonnegative sequences minimizing

    2*n*max(convolve(a,a)) / (sum(a)**2)

The main components are:
  - fast convolution-based objective,
  - a strong deterministic one-sided starting family (sqrt(1/(1-z)) coefficients),
  - a Fourier/log-softmax parameterization with gradient-based local search,
  - a stochastic pair-transfer/block-scaling simulated annealing search to
    escape local basins.

No external files or network access are used.  The final returned sequence is
the best sequence found before the time budget expires.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _next_pow2(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    s = 1
    while s < x:
        s *= 2
    return s


def _conv_sq(a):
    """Full linear convolution a * a, with tiny negative FFT errors clipped."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([0.0], dtype=np.float64)
    if n <= 96:
        c = np.convolve(a, a, mode="full")
    else:
        m = 2 * n - 1
        size = _next_pow2(m)
        fa = np.fft.rfft(a, size=size)
        c = np.fft.irfft(fa * fa, size=size)[:m]
    c[c < 0.0] = 0.0
    return c


def _objective_arr(a):
    """Scale-invariant objective for a numpy array."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size == 0:
        return float("inf")
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-12:
        return float("inf")
    c = _conv_sq(a)
    mc = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mc):
        return float("inf")
    return float(2.0 * a.size * mc / (s * s))


def _hankel_mult(p, w):
    """
    Return h_j = sum_{l=0}^{n-1} w_{j+l} p_l, j=0..n-1.
    This is the gradient-related product for weighted sum lags.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = p.size
    size = _next_pow2(3 * n - 2)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, size=size)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], size=size)
    conv = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, size=size)
    L = 2 * n - 2
    return conv[L - np.arange(n)]


def _recurrence_sequence(n):
    """
    One-sided family: coefficients are the first n terms of (1-z)^(-1/2).
    Its first n convolution coefficients can be made constant, giving F ~ pi/2.
    """
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return np.array([1.0])
    q = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    q[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, n):
        q[k] = q[k - 1] * float(2 * k - 1) / (2.0 * k)
    p = q / q.sum()
    return p


def _make_fourier_basis(n, max_freq):
    n = int(n)
    max_freq = int(max(max_freq, 1))
    max_freq = min(max_freq, max(1, n // 4))
    idx = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    cols = []
    for m in range(1, max_freq + 1):
        ang = 2.0 * np.pi * float(m) * idx / float(n)
        cols.append(np.cos(ang))
        cols.append(np.sin(ang))
    if not cols:
        return np.zeros((n, 0), dtype=np.float64)
    return np.column_stack(cols)


def _softmax_seq(v):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64)
    v = v - v.max()
    e = np.exp(v)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones_like(v) / v.size
    return e / s


def _top_unique_candidates(store, seq, score, limit=6):
    """Store a candidate if it is promising and not nearly duplicated by length."""
    seq = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
    if seq.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(score):
        return store
    n = int(seq.size)
    normed = seq / seq.sum()
    # Avoid storing several copies of the same length with very close score.
    for existing in store:
        en = int(existing[0].size)
        if en == n and score > existing[1] - 1e-8:
            return store
    store.append((normed, float(score)))
    store.sort(key=lambda x: x[1])
    if len(store) > limit:
        store = store[:limit]
    return store


def _quick_random_candidates(budget_s, seed, store):
    """Generate a relatively large number of random Dirichlet starts."""
    if budget_s <= 0:
        return store
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget_s)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    lengths = [32, 64, 128, 256, 512]
    alphas = [0.25, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0, 1.5, 2.5, 4.0]
    count_per = 120
    for n in lengths:
        for alpha in alphas:
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break
            try:
                p = rng.dirichlet(full_alpha * np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
            except Exception:
                p = rng.random(n)
                p /= p.sum()
            sc = _objective_arr(p)
            store = _top_unique_candidates(store, p, sc)
    return store


def _fourier_optimize(n, max_freq, budget_s, seed):
    """
    Optimize p_i = softmax(sum_a a_m * Fourier_mode_m(i)) with an
    Adam-like damped step and exact line search on the true objective.
    """
    n = int(n)
    B = _make_fourier_basis(n, max_freq)
    d = int(B.shape[1])
    if d == 0 or budget_s <= 0:
        return None, float("inf")

    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget_s)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_p = None
    best_score = float("inf")
    max_norm = 3.5 + 0.35 * float(max_freq)

    for restart in range(60):
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        a = rng.normal(0.0, 0.22, size=d)
        m_state = np.zeros(d, dtype=np.float64)
        v_state = np.zeros(d, dtype=np.float64)
        step = 0.12
        stale = 0
        it = 0

        while True:
            if time.time() > deadline:
                break
            v = B.dot(a)
            p = _softmax_seq(v)
            c = _conv_sq(p)
            M = float(c.max())
            score = 2.0 * float(n) * M
            if not np.isfinite(score):
                a = rng.normal(0.0, 0.2, size=d)
                stale += 10
                break
            if score < best_score:
                best_score = score
                best_p = p.copy()

            # Smooth active-set weights.  Small temperature focuses on maxima.
            temp = max(0.035, 0.45 * (1.0 - float(it) / 2500.0))
            z = c / (M * temp + 1e-15)
            z -= z.max()
            w = np.exp(z)
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if ws <= 0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
                w = c / c.sum() if c.sum() > 0 else np.ones_like(c) / c.size
            else:
                w /= ws

            h = _hankel_mult(p, w)
            gp = 2.0 * h
            mg = p * gp
            grad = B.T.dot(mg)

            if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
                stale += 50
                break

            gscale = float(np.sqrt(np.mean(grad * grad)))
            if gscale < 1e-14:
                stale += 25
                break

            m_state = 0.9 * m_state + 0.1 * grad
            v_state = 0.99 * v_state + 0.01 * (grad * grad)

            accepted = False
            last_good = None
            # Try several step sizes.  If none improves, roll back.
            for mult in (1.0, 0.55, 0.25, 0.1):
                a_try = a - (step * mult * m_state) / (np.sqrt(v_state) + 1e-8)
                nr = float(np.linalg.norm(a_try))
                if nr > max_norm:
                    a_try *= (max_norm / nr)
                v_try = B.dot(a_try)
                p_try = _softmax_seq(v_try)
                c_try = _conv_sq(p_try)
                score_try = 2.0 * float(n) * float(c_try.max())
                if np.isfinite(score_try) and score_try < score:
                    last_good = (a_try, p_try, score_try)
                    accepted = True
                    break
                if time.time() > deadline:
                    break

            if accepted and last_good is not None:
                a, p, score = last_good
                if score < best_score:
                    best_score = score
                    best_p = p.copy()
                step = min(0.55, step * 1.05)
                stale = 0
            else:
                step *= 0.72
                stale += 1
                if step < 0.004 or stale > 60:
                    break
            it += 1

        if best_score < 1.505:
            break

    return best_p, best_score


def _local_search(p, budget_s, seed):
    """
    Stochastic simulated annealing with pair transfers, block scalings,
    multiplicative gradient moves, and occasional random swaps/noise.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if p.size == 0:
        return p
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    ps = float(p.sum())
    if ps <= 1e-14:
        p = np.ones_like(p) / p.size
    else:
        p /= ps

    n = int(p.size)
    if budget_s <= 0:
        return p

    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + float(budget_s)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    current_score = _objective_arr(p)
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_score = current_score
    temperature = max(1e-5, 0.012 * best_score)
    stale = 0
    eval_count = 0

    while time.time() < deadline:
        move = rng.random()

        if move < 0.58:
            # Pair transfer: move arbitrary fraction between two coordinates.
            i, j = rng.integers(0, n, size=2)
            if i == j:
                j = (j + 1) % n
            e = float(p[i] + p[j])
            if e <= 1e-15:
                continue
            r = rng.random()
            if r < 0.55:
                lam = float(rng.random())
            elif r < 0.75:
                lam = float(p[i] / e)
                lam = max(0.0, min(1.0, lam + float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.18))))
            elif r < 0.95:
                lam = float(rng.choice(np.array([0.0, 0.01, 0.03, 0.08, 0.18,
                                                 0.33, 0.5, 0.67, 0.82, 0.92,
                                                 0.97, 0.99, 1.0])))
            else:
                lam = 1.0 - float(p[i] / e)

            cand = p.copy()
            cand[i] = lam * e
            cand[j] = e - cand[i]
            if cand.min() < 0.0:
                cand[np.where(cand < 0.0)[0]] = 0.0
            s = cand.sum()
            if s <= 1e-14:
                continue
            cand /= s

        elif move < 0.76:
            # Block scaling.
            width = int(rng.integers(2, max(3, n // 10)))
            if width >= n:
                width = max(1, n // 4)
            start = int(rng.integers(0, max(1, n - width + 1)))
            stop = min(n, start + width)
            factor = float(np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.22)))
            cand = p.copy()
            cand[start:stop] *= factor
            s = cand.sum()
            if s <= 1e-14:
                continue
            cand /= s

        elif move < 0.90:
            # Multiplicative active-set descent: p <- p * exp(-eta H_w(p)).
            c = _conv_sq(p)
            M = float(c.max())
            if M <= 0:
                continue
            temp = float(rng.uniform(0.03, 0.18))
            z = c / (M * temp)
            z -= z.max()
            w = np.exp(z)
            ws = float(w.sum())
            if ws <= 0 or not np.isfinite(ws):
                continue
            w /= ws
            h = _hankel_mult(p, w)
            HM = float(h.max())
            if HM <= 1e-15:
                continue
            eta = float(np.exp(rng.normal(-2.4, 0.7)))
            cand = p * np.exp(-eta * h / HM)
            s = cand.sum()
            if s <= 1e-14 or not np.isfinite(s):
                continue
            cand /= s

        else:
            # Random swap or tiny noise to leave very flat basins.
            cand = p.copy()
            if rng.random() < 0.55:
                i, j = rng.integers(0, n, size=2)
                if i == j:
                    j = (j + 1) % n
                cand[i], cand[j] = cand[j], cand[i]
            else:
                cand = cand + (1e-6 * rng.random(n))
                s = cand.sum()
                if s <= 1e-14:
                    continue
                cand /= s

        sc = _objective_arr(cand)
        eval_count += 1
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            temperature = max(1e-5, temperature * 0.985)
            continue

        accept = sc < current_score
        if not accept and temperature > 0.0:
            # Avoid overflow in acceptance probability.
            gap = (sc - current_score) / temperature
            if gap < 30.0:
                if np.log(rng.random() + 1e-15) > -gap:
                    accept = True

        if accept:
            current_score = sc
            p = cand
            stale = 0
            if sc < best_score:
                best_score = sc
                best_p = p.copy()
                temperature = max(1e-5, temperature * 0.985)
        else:
            stale += 1
            temperature = max(1e-5, temperature * 0.99999)
            if stale > 20000:
                break

    return best_p


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """
    Search for the best sequence and return it as a Python list of floats.
    """
    total_budget = float(max(0.0, float(budget_s)))
    t0 = time.time()
    deadline = t0 + max(0.0, total_budget - 8.0)

    store = []
    best_seq = None
    best_score = float("inf")

    def consider_seq(seq, score=None):
        nonlocal best_seq, best_score
        if seq is None:
            return
        a = np.asarray(seq, dtype=np.float64)
        if a.size == 0:
            return
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        s = float(a.sum())
        if s <= 1e-12 or not np.isfinite(s):
            return
        if score is None:
            score = _objective_arr(a)
        if not np.isfinite(score):
            return
        if score < best_score:
            best_score = score
            best_seq = a.copy()
        store = _top_unique_candidates(store, a, score)

    # 1) Safe fallbacks and strong deterministic starts.
    fallback = _recurrence_sequence(256)
    consider_seq(fallback)
    print(f"[start] recurrence n=256 score={_objective_arr(fallback):.9f}")

    for n in (32, 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024, 2048):
        if time.time() > deadline:
            break
        p = _recurrence_sequence(n)
        consider_seq(p)
        print(f"[start] recurrence n={n} score={_objective_arr(p):.9f}")

    # Optional: use the supplied previous construction if it is not too large.
    try:
        hs = height_sequence_1
    except Exception:
        hs = None
    if hs is not None and hasattr(hs, "__len__") and 0 < len(hs) <= 20000:
        try:
            h = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
            if h.size > 0 and np.all(np.isfinite(h)):
                consider_seq(h)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # 2) A few random candidates, cheaply.
    if time.time() < deadline:
        store = _quick_random_candidates(max(5.0, min(35.0, (deadline - time.time()) * 0.04)),
                                         seed=101, store=store)

    # Snapshot top candidates before expensive optimization.
    quart_get = sorted(store, key=lambda x: x[1])[:4]

    # 3) Fourier / log-softmax continuous-structure search.
    if time.time() < deadline:
        remaining = deadline - time.time()
        if best_score > 1.530 and remaining > 300:
            n1 = 1024
            f1, s1 = _fourier_optimize(n1, max_freq=4,
                                       budget_s=min(700.0, remaining * 0.55),
                                       seed=7)
            consider_seq(f1, s1)
            if f1 is not None:
                print(f"[fourier] n={n1}, freq=4 best={s1:.9f}")

        if time.time() < deadline and best_score > 1.512:
            remaining = deadline - time.time()
            f2, s2 = _fourier_optimize(512, max_freq=6,
                                       budget_s=min(480.0, remaining * 0.55),
                                       seed=17)
            consider_seq(f2, s2)
            if f2 is not None:
                print(f"[fourier] n=512, freq=6 best={s2:.9f}")

        if time.time() < deadline and best_score > 1.5027:
            remaining = deadline - time.time()
            f3, s3 = _fourier_optimize(2048, max_freq=3,
                                       budget_s=min(350.0, remaining * 0.50),
                                       seed=23)
            consider_seq(f3, s3)
            if f3 is not None:
                print(f"[fourier] n=2048, freq=3 best={s3:.9f}")

    # 4) Local stochastic search on the best few candidates.
    if time.time() < deadline:
        if best_seq is not None and best_score not in [x[1] for x in store]:
            store = _top_unique_candidates(store, best_seq, best_score)

        # Always spend some time on the current best, and on diverse strong starts.
        for base, _ in quart_get:
            if time.time() < deadline and base is not None:
                if base.size <= 256:
                    budget = max(30.0, min(260.0, (deadline - time.time()) * 0.35))
                elif base.size <= 1024:
                    budget = max(25.0, min(240.0, (deadline - time.time()) * 0.30))
                else:
                    budget = max(15.0, min(120.0, (deadline - time.time()) * 0.20))
                if budget > 5:
                    q = _local_search(base, budget, seed=31)
                    consider_seq(q)

        if best_seq is not None:
            if best_seq.size <= 512:
                budget = max(40.0, min(450.0, (deadline - time.time()) * 0.50))
            elif best_seq.size <= 2048:
                budget = max(30.0, min(300.0, (deadline - time.time()) * 0.45))
            else:
                budget = max(10.0, min(120.0, (deadline - time.time()) * 0.35))
            if budget > 5:
                q = _local_search(best_seq, budget, seed=97)
                consider_seq(q)

    # Final authoritative validation with the provided evaluator if available.
    if best_seq is None:
        best_seq = fallback
    final_list = [float(x) for x in best_seq]
    try:
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(final_list))
        if np.isfinite(val):
            best_score = min(best_score, val)
            print(f"[final] evaluator score={val:.9f}, length={len(final_list)}")
        else:
            print("[final] evaluator returned non-finite; using internal best")
    except Exception:
        print("[final] evaluator not available or failed; using internal score")

    return final_list
```
